# Mayonnaise

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="mayonnaise-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="mayonnaise-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="mayonnaise-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Mayonnaise in the HMI library has 64 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn from 6 sources.<sup <ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel and chromium and others, in 64 results from 6 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Mayonnaise, as sold from glass-packed, 4 brands x 3 production series, Iranian market had lead higher at 680 µg/kg, against 670 µg/kg for all packaging types, Iranian market in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>

with subgroup medians from 7 to 16.9 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, mayonnaise, as sold had a median lead result below 4 µg/kg across 3 composites from 2018–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Torkashvand et al. 2026 reported lead at 670 µg/kg (mean) in mayonnaise, as sold from all packaging types, Iranian market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Higher results came from contaminated or exposed settings rather than ordinary supply. Mayonnaise, as sold from sachet-packed, 4 brands x 3 production series, Iranian market had chromium higher at 640 µg/kg, against 400 µg/kg for all packaging types, Iranian market in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the mayonnaise came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-mayonnaise" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-mayonnaise-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of mayonnaise, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Mayonnaise, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤7</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤7</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>37 reference units; 3 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>33</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>3</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>Spungen et al. 2024</td><td>1</td><td>33%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for mayonnaise, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>3 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li><li><a href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" class="internal" data-slug="sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" data-no-cite-meta>Spungen et al. 2024</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for mayonnaise, oil on this basis in the completed mayonnaise ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:mayonnaise-03073b20f5f9:c5ef3b0ad4e7</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:mayonnaise-2e6a17eaecab:d3afb0f791ad"><summary>HMI mayonnaise U reference · Mayonnaise, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:mayonnaise-2e6a17eaecab:d3afb0f791ad"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI mayonnaise U reference · Mayonnaise, as sold. Mayonnaise, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤1</td><td>≤1</td><td>≤1</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤1</td><td>≤1</td><td>≤1</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>3 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>3</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for mayonnaise, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>FDA Total Diet Study is the only compatible sampling family for mayonnaise, as sold on this basis in the completed mayonnaise ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:mayonnaise-2e6a17eaecab:d3afb0f791ad</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><div hidden data-form-reasons><p data-reason="bounds_span_categories" data-title="Non-detect spans the bands">The result is below a reporting limit, and the range from zero to that limit crosses more than one band.</p><p data-reason="species_or_material_without_compatible_reference_after_full_ledger" data-title="No reference for this form">No medians or sample values exist for this form and metal, so no HMI reference could be built.</p><p data-reason="reference_below_display_minimum_after_full_ledger" data-title="Reference too small">The HMI reference for this form has fewer than four units or no measurable spread, below the display minimum.</p><p data-reason="basis_incompatible_with_all_references" data-title="Basis has no reference">The result is on a basis (dry weight, or not stated) that no HMI reference for this form uses, so it cannot be placed.</p><p data-reason="bounded" data-title="Limits overlap">Reporting limits overlap the study comparison, so no direction can be shown.</p></div><script type="application/octet-stream" Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI mayonnaise Pb reference · Mayonnaise, as sold. median as reported: ≤12.8 µg/kg. HMI median ≤4; Q1 ≤4; Q3 ≤7 µg/kg. 3 families. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without WHO GEMS/Food: Limited data.</li><li>Without FDA Total Diet Study: Limited data.</li><li>Without Spungen et al. 2024: Limited data.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:mayonnaise-424383e7d904:3e5a6470c1ee"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:mayonnaise-424383e7d904:3e5a6470c1ee">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2003–2018</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2022–2023</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>Fats and oils (retail, Mexico City)</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Product category within one study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>Dehydration of solids at 80 C for 72 h, HNO3 digestion, graphite-furnace AAS (PerkinElmer AAnalyst-600).</p><p>Below the limit of detection</p><p>Below LoQ 0.0025 mg/kg; table prints non-detects as &lt;LoQ (cell partly garbled in converted text; non-detect status inferred from the 19 detected items listed in the Results).</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>not_detected</td><td>&lt;LoQ (0.0025 mg/kg)</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>Table 1, Fats and oils, Mayonnaise (Brand 1).</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach mayonnaise

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

The evidence on how metals reach mayonnaise comes from studies that compared exposed and ordinary settings. Mayonnaise, as sold from sachet-packed, 4 brands x 3 production series, Iranian market had chromium higher at 640 µg/kg, against 400 µg/kg for all packaging types, Iranian market in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

These are observational comparisons from single studies; the size of the difference depends on the site, metal and distance.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The HMI reference for lead in mayonnaise, as sold pools 37 subgroup units from 3 sampling families, with a median of below 4 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 4 (lower quartile) and below 7 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium in mayonnaise, as sold, the reference median was below 1 µg/kg, with a middle half from below 1 to below 1 µg/kg across 26 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For total arsenic in mayonnaise, as sold, the reference median was below 3 µg/kg, with a middle half from below 3 to below 5 µg/kg across 12 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, mayonnaise, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in EURO (7 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (16.9 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for mayonnaise

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Avoiding exposed growing areas lowered the metal measured in the studies available, but no study measured the cost or supply effect of doing so.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing mayonnaise for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>

<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 1 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 4,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-032"></a>
<a id="how-do-consumption-patterns-and-intended-populations-affect-dietary-exposure-interpretation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-033"></a>
<a id="what-conclusions-require-finished-product-measurements-or-a-separate-exposure-assessment"></a>

No source in the library converted mayonnaise concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.


<a id="why-this-commodity-accumulates-heavy-metals"></a>

<a id="requirements-evidence-assessment-and-updates"></a>
## Evidence review and remaining gaps

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>
<a id="which-primary-regulatory-instruments-apply-to-this-exact-ingredient-form-species-market-and-date"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-035"></a>
<a id="what-searches-eligibility-decisions-source-families-and-appraisal-underpin-the-answers-and-certainty-judgments"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-036"></a>
<a id="what-remains-unresolved-when-was-each-answer-reviewed-and-what-changed-in-this-version"></a>

Every source record that names mayonnaise was decided on its content: 6 occurrence extracted, 6 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Mayonnaise FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-mayonnaise" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-mayonnaise" data-question="What heavy metals are found in mayonnaise?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in mayonnaise?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn in mayonnaise, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in mayonnaise, as sold from 159 results in 2 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 7 to 16.9 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-do-heavy-metals-get-into-mayonnaise" data-anchor="how-do-heavy-metals-get-into-mayonnaise" data-question="How do heavy metals get into mayonnaise?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How do heavy metals get into mayonnaise?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In the studies available, mayonnaise from contaminated or exposed settings carried more metal than mayonnaise from the same study’s control.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Mayonnaise, as sold from glass-packed, 4 brands x 3 production series, Iranian market had lead higher at 680 µg/kg, against 670 µg/kg for all packaging types, Iranian market in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Torkashvand J, Torkashvand S, Tamjidi F · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Effect of transitional nutrition in intake level of heavy metal from Iranian sauces consumption and its carcinogenic and health risks</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports (Article in Press, accepted 29 September 2026)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/torkashvand2026-iran-sauces-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-mayonnaise" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-mayonnaise" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in mayonnaise?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in mayonnaise?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, mayonnaise, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in EURO (7 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (16.9 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
